<!-- ideas-index:end -->

**A result here is a prior about the Arena, not a verdict.** The Arena's products, bots, matching and scoring will differ from Prosperity's. So an idea that loses here stays on this list, marked `dropped here`, and is tested again on the Arena's data. An idea that wins here is tested again too. `ON_THE_DAY.md` ("Every round") points here.

**Keeping it up to date:**
- When a test runs, add the result to its row: the date, the data, the command and the numbers. Keep the earlier results and add the new one under them.
- After any edit to a row, `uv run python -m experiments.ideas_index` rewrites the index at the top (`python/tests/test_ideas_index.py` fails until it is current).
- Each verdict is one of these:
  - `kept`: it is in the template;
  - `dropped here`: it is not in the template, and is re-tested on the day anyway;
  - `measured`: a fact about the data, not a strategy;
  - `open`: tested, with no conclusion yet.
- **`--resting` results before 2026-10-13's review fixes are a little high.** Until then a resting order crossed by the next snapshot could also fill from that tick's market trades at the same price, which used the same orders twice. The fix took 0.3–3.6% off the template alone (P3 round 1: 68,043 to 67,446) and 13–16% off its runs against copies of itself. The rows below that were re-run say so; older `--resting` numbers elsewhere were not re-run.
- On the day, for each round, go down the "Re-test on the Arena" column and run the rows whose condition fits that round's products, again in every round: the rounds get harder, so an Arena result from an earlier round is a prior too.

**What a market we didn't write said about these priors** (2026-10-27; ABIDES's RMSC04, 10 days, "An independent generator: ABIDES's RMSC04" and the rows after it; LOBSTER's real book is still to come, `docs/MANUAL_TODO.md`):
- **Held:** the statistics and the tools. The grid's pick rule, the held-out checks and StepM named the settings that also won on held-out ABIDES days; `diagnose`'s notes read the market correctly (large-tick, trades move the wall mid, a thin margin); the fill-model spread (`all` against `worse` against resting) mattered as much as there.
- **Did not hold:** the playbook. Prosperity's one wall at the value, its uninformed flow and its free inventory are what make plain market making pay. In an agent market with informed and momentum traders, no fair mode finds the value (all 34 ticks off), passive fills lose to adverse selection, moves continue, and every quoting setting of the template as committed loses with resting orders (doing nothing beats it). The shuffle test tells the two apart in one command: shuffling Prosperity's ticks changes nothing, shuffling ABIDES's turns a loss into millions.
- **What paid there instead:** a fair that leans on the flow and the trend (`lin_rho3` 3, `lin_momentum` 0.5 over 100 ticks: +1.1M to +1.2M under every replay mode, and +1.27M inside the market itself, where the template as committed loses 230k; re-run 2026-10-28, the fourth audit's R13 and R14), or pulling the side a move runs over (`gate_move` 2 over 50 ticks: +0.7M); both cost a little on Prosperity, so both stay off by default. Smoothing, per-side edges, a take reserve, the hysteretic mid and the join gate did not help.
- **So on the day:** run the shuffle test and `quote_markouts` on the first download before trusting any row below. If the Arena's market looks like Prosperity's, these rows stand as priors; if it looks like ABIDES's, start from the linear fair and the gate, and from a bot that does nothing as the benchmark to beat.

## Tested on Prosperity data

| Idea | Result on Prosperity data | Verdict here | Re-test on the Arena |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The template's market maker** (take, clear, make; fixed fair for Resin, `wall_mid` for Kelp) | P3 round 1, all days: 81,292 per tick with `--match-trades all`, 57,774 with `worse`, 28,216 with `none`; 68,043 with `--resting` (`README.md`, "Fill assumptions matter") <br>2026-10-13, after the review's `--resting` fix: 67,446 with `--resting`, 34,968 with `--resting --fill-ratio 0.5`; the per-tick numbers are unchanged | `kept` | The first backtest on the Arena's data, in every fill mode, then compare with a playground run (`ON_THE_DAY.md`, "Which backtest settings match the playground?") |
| **Kelp `make_edge` and `take_edge`** | P3 round 1 grid: `make_edge=1, take_edge=1` best by PnL, neighbourhood and worst day under `all` (81,292); `make_edge=0` best under `worse` (63,068). With `--resting`, `make_edge=0` loses 12,474 against one copy of the baseline (`README.md`) <br>2026-10-13, after the review's fixes (`make` keeps the bid below the ask; `--resting` no longer fills twice): `make_edge=0` best under `worse` with 63,054. With `--resting` it loses 4,920 against one copy of the baseline (10,980 under `worse`; `uv run python -m arena.backtest python/bots/template.py 1 --resting --set PARAMS.KELP.make_edge=0 --vs python/bots/template.py`). Alone under `--resting --match-trades worse` it is still best: 62,636 against 57,958 for `make_edge=1` <br>**2026-10-23, six settings at once, 100,000 cells** (Kelp's `make_edge` 0.5–5 by 0.5, `take_edge` 0–9, `clear_edge` 0–4, `skew` 0–9; Resin's `make_edge` 1–4, `take_edge` 0–4; P3 round 1, `--modes all,worse`, `uv run python -m arena.grid python/bots/template.py 1 --grid PARAMS.KELP.make_edge=0.5,1,1.5,2,2.5,3,3.5,4,4.5,5 --grid PARAMS.KELP.take_edge=0..9 --grid PARAMS.KELP.clear_edge=0..4 --grid PARAMS.KELP.skew=0..9 --grid PARAMS.RAINFOREST_RESIN.make_edge=1..4 --grid PARAMS.RAINFOREST_RESIN.take_edge=0..4 --modes all,worse --engine rust`, about 5 minutes): against the template as committed (72,674 / 63,302), the best under `all` is Kelp 1.5 / 1 / 0 / 0 with Resin 2 / 1, 84,512 (52,294 under `worse`), and the best under `worse` is Kelp 0.5 / 1 / 0 / 0 with Resin `make_edge` 1, 65,964 (70,132 under `all`), also the best worst case of the two. Any `skew` or `clear_edge` above 0 only loses (the best cell at `skew` 9 makes 71,378). StepM finds 108 cells that beat the template under `all` and none under `worse`, so the pick stays the template as is; DSR 0.19 (4,896 distinct cells). **Six settings at once tell the same as two:** the fill mode decides the edge | `kept`: `make_edge=1` | Grid each product's edges with `--modes all,worse`; add `--vs` if the market is shared. Many settings at once: `--engine rust`, with `--halving 0.5` (and `--sample N` past a few hundred thousand cells) |

| **`skew` against Kelp's long position** | Kelp sits at +50 about 10% of the time, and `skew` didn't fix it; no numbers recorded (`docs/TODO_DONE.md`, "Explain Kelp's long drift") <br>2026-10-26, `uv run python -m experiments.study.kelp_drift` (P3 round 1, three days, the template as committed): <br>- **The position is the takers' flow, not a bias.** On each day mirrored (prices reflected, bids for asks, buyers for sellers), Kelp's mean position is exactly the negative of the day as is (+21.5 to +35.2 becomes −21.5 to −35.2) and the PnL is the same to the unit, under `all`, `worse` and `--resting`; Resin's small positions flip the same way. So the template's fair and quotes are symmetric, and the long comes from the flow: 34–36% of Kelp's trades at the touch are buys (41–43% of the volume), against 49–50% on Resin. Under `all` our passive buys exceed our passive sells 1.24–1.32 to 1 (3,930–4,060 lots against 3,041–3,247 a day), and takes sell most of it back (1,760–1,821 against 829–1,010). <br>- **The flow isn't informed** (Harris's test): our position's correlation with the mark's change over the next 10–1,000 ticks is −0.10 to +0.18 over the days and modes, mostly positive; Resin +0.02 to +0.06. <br>- **`skew` costs PnL at every step**, three days, skew 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 ticks at the full limit: `all` 18,698 / 18,210 / 17,350 / 15,893 (mean position +24.5 / +19.3 / +13.9 / +9.7); `worse` 14,530 / 12,370 / 10,498 / 9,514 (+26.4 / +14.1 / +7.0 / +5.1); `--resting` 16,834 / 13,208 / 10,298 / 8,096 (+31.3 / +14.9 / +6.9 / +4.7). Holding the position is what earning on one-sided, uninformed flow costs, and here it costs nothing measurable | `dropped here` (default 0.0) | Sweep `skew` per product, using the coefficient the literature gives (`references/wiki/concepts/optimal-quoting-models.md`). A product held on one side where `arena.diagnose`'s buy share is far from half is the flow, not a bias: skew it only if Harris's test on the `--out` timeline is clearly negative |

| **What the P4 vouchers are** (implied volatility per strike, and what the teams' voucher PnL is made of) | 2026-10-13, `uv run python -m experiments.day.p4_vouchers` and `experiments.study.p4_levels`, the four distinct days: <br>- **Volatility is flat within a day:** on the strikes with time value (5000–5500), the median implied vol is 0.245–0.278, its intraday IQR 0.003–0.009, and the first and last tenth of the day agree within 0.015. It creeps up across days (VEV_5000: 0.259, 0.262, 0.270, 0.275). VEV_5400 sits 0.013–0.021 below its neighbours every day. <br>- **Each voucher is Velvetfruit with a fixed delta:** its wall mid minus Black–Scholes at that day's median vol has an sd of 0.3–0.4 on 5000 and 0.4–1.1 on 5100–5200, under half the spread; on 5300–5500, with one- and two-tick spreads, 0.3–1.1, about half the spread. 4000 and 4500 trade at exactly intrinsic (delta 1), 6000 and 6500 at 0 / 1. <br>- **The teams' voucher PnL is leveraged Velvetfruit:** Baguette Fromage's carry on 5000–5500 is 697k, of which 690k is delta × Velvetfruit's move; on 4000–4500 all of it (416k). Alone it costs −2.5k under `all`, +2.7k under `worse`, −0.5k with `--resting` (`uv run python -m arena.backtest python/bots/template.py 1 --match-trades worse --set PARAMS.RAINFOREST_RESIN.make_edge=1`, and `all`; the decision is in `docs/TODO_DONE.md`). <br>- **Found on the way: two copies of Pepper Root's drift bet fight over it.** Per tick, one copy holds +80 all day and the other's `stop_loss` (4.5k) fires within 60 ticks, every day (worst day −5.3k; which one wins is the arrival shuffle). Without the stop both swing between +80 and −70 and lose (day −2: 59.5k and −11.4k against 77.4k alone). At its target the template offers its whole position and 80 more (`make_size` None) one tick above fair, and a rival below the same target bids up to that price with its skew, so the full room changes hands in one tick. Pepper Root's figures above are with its full room; since its `make_size` 10 (next row) a copy costs it 32.2k / 20.9k / 3.0k under `all` / `worse` / `--resting`, and both copies hold the bet (429.5k and 432.4k under `all`) <br>**2026-10-23, after the per-tick priority fix** (`e003e81`: per tick the bots' leftover orders now meet the market trades best price first, where before they met them in arrival order; before/after on the same code, `--engine rust`): per tick the undercutter now costs a little more than a plain copy under `all`: Kelp −9.0k against −8.4k (our passive share 44% against 48%), Resin −25.4k against −22.9k (41% against 51%), Osmium −55.5k against −55.3k; under `worse` they are level again (Resin −24.6k both). Quoting at `make_edge` 0 ourselves wins back at most 1.1k (Resin, against the undercutter). Pepper Root with `make_size` 10 against a copy: −32.9k / −21.0k under `all` / `worse`. Every `--resting` figure above is unchanged | `measured`: undercutting by a tick costs no more than any rival per tick; with resting orders, a fixed fair at `make_edge` 2 is the one setting that loses everything to it | On the day, if orders rest and a product's fair is known exactly (Resin-like), quote it at `make_edge` 1, not 2. If our fill share on the playground falls well below the share our PnL implies, check for a rival at our price. Run `experiments.day.undercut --data <folder> --days <days> --war <a fixed-fair product>` |

Its lag-1 autocorrelation is −0.04: it is gone by the next tick, so only taking catches it. <br>- **Traded with the template's new `smile` fair** (Black–Scholes at the smile from the other days, plus the voucher's EMA offset from it; take, clear and make around that), rock and vouchers only, thousands over the five days: take-only at `take_edge` 0.5 on 9750 and 10000: **528.8, every day positive (worst +18.2), the same under `all`, `worse` and `--resting`**; on all five strikes 457.8 (worst −8.4); higher take edges make less (427.0 at 1, 255.4 at 2). Quoting as well (`make_edge` 1) adds 30.6 under `all` (559.4; 526.0 under `worse`) but loses 0.6–2.5M with `--resting`: the rock moves and the next snapshot crosses the stale quotes, the jump lesson again. The template as committed (the vouchers follow the rock's level bet) makes 155.4 with a worst day of −189.9. Chosen on the other four days and scored on the fifth: 505.2 (the quoting variant every time). <br>- **The smile is not what pays:** one volatility for every strike (the fit's value at m = 0) makes 567.0 against 559.4; no option model (plain market making on each voucher) loses 76.4. The edge is pricing each voucher off the rock: the vouchers lag the rock's moves *(inferred from the controls)*. <br>- **The delta hedge halves it:** `hedge_ratio` 1 (the rock holds minus the vouchers' delta, crossing up to 1) costs 49.9–256.4 on the rock in every variant, and no day gains from it. <br>- P2 round 4's coconut coupon: "Prosperity 2 round 4's coconut coupons with the template's options modes" below (2026-11-02): there the residual drifts (lag-1 0.89), and `smile` pays only with a `smile_window` of thousands of ticks | `kept` as a template mode (`smile`, `underlying`, `expiry`, `smile_window`), take-only; the smile's shape `dropped here` (a flat vol does as well), the delta hedge `dropped here`. `PARAMS` unchanged: the vouchers' expiry differs on every backtest day | On the day, for options on a product: fit `v(m)` (part 1 of the script, or one vol), give each option `smile`, `underlying`, `strike`, `expiry` (days at the round's open), `take_edge` 0.5 and `make_size` 0 (quotes only if orders don't rest); trade the strikes whose fast residual is larger than half their spread (part 2), and grid `smile_window` (20 to 20,000: P3's residual reverts within a tick, P2's coupon's over thousands). | **Takers that recur across days** (Baguette Fromage, P4 round 2: takers "reappeared at identical timestamps, with identical side, and with identical size, on consecutive days", 97.7% of Osmium's of size 7 and up, none in round 1 (claims), `references/prosperity4-baguette-fromage/README.md:557-668`; an audit's research pass, 2026-10-26, idea 1) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.day.repeats` (about 15 s): per product, the share of day d's (timestamp, side, size) market trades that day d + 1 has too, the side read from the same tick's book (at or above the ask a buy), against day d + 1 shifted by 20 random offsets. <br>- **P4 round 2:** Osmium's trades of size 7 and up recur on 103/105 = 98.1% and 105/108 = 97.2% (all sizes 72.5% and 42.9%), against a baseline of 0.1–0.3%; Pepper Root's on 57.5% and 76.3% (all sizes 53.8% and 73.8%). The template quotes all the room under its limit, so one tick late it races on 3,248 of day 0's 10,000 ticks (the second audit's follow-up, 2026-10-26). **Capping the quotes** (`--delay 1 --grid PARAMS.KELP.make_size=None,40,30,20,15,10`, the same for Resin, `--engine rust`): 15–20 is best for both on every measure and on each held-out day, Kelp +2,228, Resin +2,946; both at 20 make 45,534 / 34,282 against 40,472 / 31,218, with 1,001 race ticks on day 0, and 72,670 / 63,306 on time against 72,674 / 63,302. | **The kickoff prompts on a disguised round** (the second audit's A2: an agent-only mock round; `KICKOFF_PROMPT.md`'s repo prompt in an isolated copy of the repo, and its prompt without the repository in an empty folder) | 2026-10-26, `python/experiments/mock_round/` (README: rebuild and score in seconds): three `arena.sim` products disguised as CALDERA (jumps of 6.00 that snap back 84% of the time, limit 15), LUMEN (returns to a level, limit 120) and OBERON (a market maker that doesn't know the price, tick 0.02, limit 60), with a function API and orders that rest; 3 public days, 2 hidden ones scored by `private/evaluate.py` (our engine, `--resting`, `worse`). <br>- **The repo run** validated a bot 7 minutes in and finished in 18: 65,351 on the public days, **42,446 on the hidden ones** (about 21k a session on both). Against the round's market maker's 8,632 and its reference bot's 12,195. So the restructure did not make a fresh session faster or cheaper: reading cost was never the bottleneck, the rules' mapping onto the engine was (both runs spent most of their thought there). Both named the same gaps: the engine settings a format's rules fix (fees, queue, mark) can't live in the plug-in, `premise` takes none of them, the engine has no cap on all actions or on resting orders, its limit rule differs from a per-order one, and the trades' tick convention was found by reading `run_market` (the checklist now states it; the rest is in `TODO.md`, "From the 2026-10-28 rehearsal"). The after-run also found the restructure's own faults, fixed before the commit: ON_THE_DAY's size test left no room for the day's answers, the kickoff asked for `premise` before the bot existed, and "Formats are plug-ins" was cited as a section though it is a paragraph. **Why the after-run scored 805 less on the hidden days:** VANTA, 775 of it (−491 against +284), from one ADAPTER choice and not from the restructure: where the template's top-ups would pass the 4-resting cap, the after-run's ADAPTER pulls every order of the product and posts afresh, losing its queue place on a large-tick book, while the before-run's drops the posts that don't fit. With only that changed (`python/experiments/mock_round/round2/results/after/template_drop_excess.py`) it makes 9,708 hidden, 69 short of the before-run's 9,777, and 12,062 public. <br>- **The engine fit, before and after** (2026-10-30, `python/experiments/mock_round/round3/`: the Kestrel Bourse, rules that differ from the engine on purpose, four of them unstated: `worse` matching, the last-trade mark, orders a step late, the queue; a portal giving the Arena's metrics and a results ZIP; stopped once a validated bot was uploaded and the engine fitted to it): **before** (24a536a: no `ENGINE`, caps or calibrate) uploaded at T+8 and had every rule right by T+11, by reading the ZIP's timestamps and refusals and matching fills, volume, penalty and score by hand, the latency held in its own wrapper; about 15 minutes (12:07 to 12:22 by its log), 95 responses, 15.2M tokens (307k without cache reads). **After** (d586efd) uploaded at T+36, and `experiments.day.calibrate --vary delay=0,1` fitted all twelve portal figures at 0.0% within a minute (circular, the audit's round 8 found: the mock portal scores with our own engine; against the round's `mm_bot`, whose cancels its wrapper and the portal handle differently, the best fit was 30% off and wrong on delay, and calibrate still printed an ENGINE. Since 2026-11-03 it refuses above 5% on the worst figure and names the cause, and tries delay 0 and 1 by default) (`worse`, `last`, delay 1; the queue can't be told apart under `worse`); 41 minutes, 53 responses, 7.3M tokens (246k): the half hour went on writing the ADAPTER for a function API, one response cut off. So calibrate turned the fit from a few minutes of insight into one command and halved the tokens counted with cache reads (15.2M to 7.3M; without them 307k to 246k, a fifth less), but the run's length was set by the ADAPTER. **Both uploads lost** (public −4,850 and −283 credits; hidden −3,553 and −489, against the round's plain market maker's +325): with orders a step late, neither ADAPTER counted the orders in flight, so each re-posted every step, hit the 3-order cap and overfilled its limits (`TODO.md`, "From the 2026-10-30 rehearsal"). **With the orders on their way counted** (2026-10-31, the template's `DELAY`; `python/experiments/mock_round/round3/results/in_flight/template.py`, the after-run's bot with only that changed, scored as the README says): public −283 → **+142**, hidden −489 → **−389**, the breach penalty 25 → 0 (`private/check_configs.py BOT hidden`: TALC 352 → 425, OPHIR −31 → −33, BRAM −811 → −782). What is left is BRAM: under the round's `worse` matching a one-tick book's quote at the touch fills only when the book moves through it, so quoting it at all loses (the round's market maker gets no BRAM fills); a PARAMS choice, not the ADAPTER's. A stricter variant that also held the room of the resting orders cancelled in the same call (an order may fill before its cancel lands) made −299 hidden, but only by quoting less of BRAM: TALC fell to 160, and on P3 round 1 day 0 (`--resting --delay 1 --max-resting 3`) it made 10,661 against 13,340, so it was dropped here. <br>- **The fourth, the Varde Exchange** (2026-11-03, at `bc8569c`, `python/experiments/mock_round/round4/README.md`; the audit's round 8 asked for a portal that isn't `arena.engine`): its portal matches and scores in its own code, with three rules outside calibrate's grid (half of each print for resting orders, a 100-print VWAP mark, a fee per order placed, stated). A JSON-lines layout, a class API with exchange ids, cents on a 5-cent tick. The round-1 kickoff prompt, `claude -p`, alone: a validated bot uploaded at T+0:07 and the whole run 14 minutes (reading only the sections RUNBOOK names). **calibrate refused** (no setting within 5%; the best 98% off on the score) and the session pasted nothing, widened `--modes` and rebuilt the score from the ZIP by hand: the order fee was 28% of the gross, and our engine filled over twice the portal's volume under every setting. Re-run afterwards with `--vary fill_ratio=0.5,1`, the fills come within 2.6% and the volume within 10.5% (34% at best without it), the half-print rule's nearest stand-in; calibrate now prints the setting closest on the fills and suggests `fill_ratio` where ours trade more under most settings. Its bot scored €208.77 on the hidden sessions, the organisers' example €80.10. What it found, filed in `TODO.md`, "From the 2026-11-03 rehearsal": no per-order fee in the engine; calibrate's `--help` crashed on a `%` (fixed); `markPrice` read as the traded products' mean only (now every product's too); the ZIP has no time scale for `fill_curve`; the qminers ADAPTER's limit comment capped a larger limit at DEFAULTS' 50 (fixed); EXECUTION's top-ups multiply orders where each costs a fee | `kept`: run both on the day, the clean-room one as a check: neither the repo nor the tools were what limited the score, the reading of each product was. What tripped them is fixed (TODO_DONE, "A2") | A human mock round, if there's time; on the day, compare the two runs' bots on the public days before choosing what to upload |

The calculator reads persistence 0.52, the bet 35% of a team's variance, our loading −0.10 (true 0). **Whether less risk keeps the lead depends on our round's Sharpe**, with the mean and sd scaling together: our round at mean 35k, sd 11k: the chance of losing first at 10/20/30/40 is 37% at k 0.25, 22% at 0.5, 6% at 1, 2% at 1.5 (keep all the risk); at mean 10k, sd 19k (`--ours=-15000,0,10000,20000,35000`): 52%, 47%, 40%, 36% (the teams behind expect more than we do, so we need the mean); at mean 35k, sd 49k (`--ours=-30000,10000,35000,60000,100000`): 40%, 32%, 27%, 25%, but the mean rank rises from 1.59 to 2.28 to 3.00, and on the equal normalised score k 0.5 is best (19%, mean rank 1.20). (Re-run 2026-10-28 under `5e6023d`'s default, the field's growth spread measured from the board; the row's first figures, 35/20/6/2%, 50/45/39/36% and 38/31/27/26%, are those of `--growth-sd 0`: the fourth audit's R12.) With the mean fixed and only the sd scaling (`--mean-power 0`, the test's two-team field) less risk always protects a lead and more always chases one, as it should. The relative hedge: at k 1, holding 0.5–1 of the field's bet cut the chance of losing a place by 2 points (seed 4, us 1st: 6% to 4% under PnL at 10/20/30/40, 5% to 3% under the score); 2nd and 12.8k behind (seed 0), the chance is 51 / 45 / 55 / 37% under the four scorings and the hedge moves it by at most 2 points. A small insurance with the bet free to hold (`--hedge-edge` 0). (The row's earlier seed-0 figures, 8 / 5 / 10 / 4% to 6 / 3 / 7 / 1%, come out of no variant re-run: R12.) <br>- **Checked on a real field** (2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.day.standings_check --rounds4 20`): the template and the 12 non-local zoo bots, each a team trading alone; rounds 1–3 P3 round 1's days, round 4 one of 20 fresh `arena.sim` days, every other one with a regime change, so 20 round 4s played. The field's spread grew from 15,211 in round 3 to 19,370 in round 4 (×1.27), which no leaderboard shows: from the leaderboards alone the calculator predicted the mean rank well (5.01 against 5.25 realised under 10/20/30/40) but the chance of losing a place far too low (13% against 40%; at half the template's limit 23% against 65%). Given the round's scale, it gets closer under PnL at 10/20/30/40: with the scale the zoo itself showed on 5 held-out simulated days (×1.31, now `--growth-from`), the mean rank 5.65 against 5.25 and losing a place 60% against 40% (half the limit: 5.84 against 5.60, 74% against 65%), a little pessimistic, with a standard error of about 11 points over 20 rounds. Under the other scorings the scale makes it worse (the fourth audit's re-run, R12): PnL summed, losing a place 8% unscaled and 46% scaled against 10% realised; the equal normalised score, 7% and 39% against 0%. So `--growth-from` is supported under the weighted PnL scoring only, and the Arena's rule is unknown; first place and the top 3 were 0% predicted and realised throughout. A random scale (`--growth-sd` 0.5) gets losing a place closer (33%) but invents upside that didn't happen (19% top 3 against 0%). And the multiplier is the weak part: halving the limit cost the template almost no mean PnL (25,410 against 25,822), where `k` 0.5 assumes half (6.81 predicted against 5.60 realised); the variant's own backtests, fed as `--ours`, predicted it. In the field (all seven at once) the template makes 19.6 and the Opus market maker 41.4. <br>- **In `arena.sim`** (seeds 0–2): template 76.8, Opus market maker 88.0, Opus baseline 72.5. <br>- **What they do alike** (read from the files): 5 of 6 hard-code Resin at 10,000 from the spec's hint; 4 of 6 use the wall mid (the largest level each side) as fair; all market makers do take, clear at fair, then penny the best quote one tick inside; inventory skew is linear or a step at a soft limit; none imports numpy or pandas; all keep state in `traderData`; none errors or breaches a limit. That is the Prosperity 3 winners' playbook: the models know it. <br>- **Where they fail:** the momentum bot waits for a 2-sd trend that never comes (Optiver's "too conservative"); the mean-reversion and Haiku bots lose 26–31k on Squid Ink fading its trend. <br>- **2026-10-15, the first bots from other providers** (baseline prompt, by hand; `experiments.study.zoo` and `experiments.study.zoo_h2h`; the template is now 72.7 / 63.3 alone after Kelp's `make_edge` 0.5): ChatGPT GPT-5.6 Luna 41.7 / 30.7, `--resting` 20.3; Gemini 3.8 Flash (thinking high) −58.7 / −82.3, `--resting` 23.5. **Neither uses the wall mid: both build their fair from the best-level weighted mid**, the option that lost on every product here ("The weighted mid as fair value"), so the claim above that the herd shares the template's price holds only for the Claude bots. Both lose on Squid Ink: Luna −19.9k (a momentum-tilted fair, taking 21k units at −1.21 against the wall mid), Gemini −124.3k (a trend-follower that crosses the spread: 95k units at −1.67). **The template beats both head to head:** 47.9 against Luna's 15.8, and 91.2 against Gemini's −136.8, where the template's Kelp makes 62.4 and Gemini's −41.5: Gemini's Kelp makes 17.4 alone, so in a shared book its weighted mid follows the template's quotes and it trades against them at a loss (its Kelp takes at −0.66). With Squid Ink traded the template takes 96k (edge 1) to 176k (edge 0.5) of it from Gemini. In the field of nine the template makes 25.6 (was 19.6), Opus market maker 105.9, Gemini −243.5. <br>- **gpt-oss-120B** (baseline, by hand): no fair value at all, it joins the best bid and ask with 10 lots. **59.7 under `all` but 0.6 under `worse` and 15.1 with `--resting`, 9.5 in `arena.sim`**: nearly all its profit is trades printed at exactly its price, which only `--match-trades all` gives it. The clearest case yet of a result that is only the fill model. Head to head the template wins 66.7 to 20.1 (73.7 to −8.8 under `worse`). In the field of ten the template makes 33.2, Opus market maker 117.8, gpt-oss −89.1, Gemini −205.7. <br>- **The `market_maker` prompt for the same three** (alone `all` / `worse`, `--resting`): Gemini 85.2 / 51.1, 39.6 (its baseline was −58.7: the prompt turned a trend chaser into a pennying market maker with a weighted-mid fair); Luna 25.4 / 3.1, 29.2 (worse than its baseline: an EMA-plus-momentum fair loses 36.4k on Squid Ink); gpt-oss −72.9 / −76.5, 0.8 (a 20-tick average of the mid on every product, Squid Ink −120.0k, and **10,280 ticks breach a position limit**: its takes and quote share the same room; `arena.validate` fails it). **Gemini's market maker is the first non-Claude bot to beat the committed template head to head under `all`: 52.3 to 45.2, all of it Squid Ink (14.9k to 0).** Under `worse` the template wins (36.6 to 27.7), and with Squid Ink quoted at 0.5 it wins under both (79.8 to −2.6; 59.0 to −8.5). The template beats the other two head to head in every variant. In the field of thirteen: Opus market maker 131.1, Opus baseline 111.6, Sonnet 61.3, template 33.2, Gemini market maker 15.6, gpt-oss market maker −60.4, gpt-oss baseline −79.8, Gemini baseline −212.0 <br>- **2026-10-17, eleven bots from local models** (`python/bots/zoo/local.py` on llama.cpp, `python/bots/zoo/LOCAL_MODELS.md`; `baseline` and `market_maker`, two samples each for gemma4:26b and gpt-oss:20b, two baselines and one market maker for qwen3.8:27b at reasoning effort medium; `experiments.study.zoo` and `experiments.study.zoo_h2h`). Alone `all` / `worse`: qwen3.8 baseline −337.9 / −342.3 and −161.2 / −161.2, market maker 35.4 / 5.6; gemma4 baseline 7.7 / −1.2 and **−575.6** / −557.5, market maker 18.4 / 17.1 and **−1,979.5** / −1,950.6; gpt-oss:20b baseline −364.6 / −364.6 and 0 (it never trades), market maker **−919.5** / −888.2 and 23.5 / 9.5. <br>- **Every file passed `arena.validate`, with no repair turn, and none comes near the template.** The best local bot (qwen3.8's market maker, 35.4) makes half the template's 72.7. **The failures are new kinds:** <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *Takers that pay the spread:* both qwen3.8 baselines sum momentum, imbalance and z-score terms and cross the spread on the result. gpt-oss:20b's baseline buys 1 at the ask and sells 1 at the bid every tick, and its first market maker bids at the best ask and asks at the best bid when flat. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *Flips between the full limits* (gemma4 baseline 2): an SMA crossover and a z-score, each reached by crossing the spread. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *A units bug* (gemma4 market maker 2): an inventory skew of `pos × mid × 0.001`, 10 ticks a lot on Resin, sends its quotes through the book after every fill. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *An ordering bug* (gpt-oss:20b baseline 2): it saves this tick's mid before reading the last one, so it never trades. <br>- **The same model and prompt can give a bot that makes money or one that loses millions:** gemma4's market maker made 18.4 and −1,979.5, gpt-oss:20b's 23.5 and −919.5. One sample says little about a model. <br>- **The template beats every local bot head to head, under both modes, and the disasters pay it:** with gpt-oss:20b's market maker in its market it makes 830.5 (alone 72.7), with gemma4's second market maker 585.2, with gemma4's second baseline 273.7 (463.6 with Squid Ink quoted). Its gains are passive fills against their crossing orders (138k Resin units at +3.18 against the wall mid next to gemma4's market maker 2). In the field of 24 the template makes 186.5 (33.2 in the field of thirteen; now `experiments.study.zoo --local`) <br>- **2026-10-18, nine more local bots** (phi4:14b and deepseek-r1:14b on both prompts twice, qwen3.8's second market maker; same commands). Alone `all` / `worse`: phi4 baseline −4.4 / 0.0 and −0.1 / 0.0, market maker 37.4 / 15.1 and −0.5 / −0.6; deepseek-r1 baseline 0 (a `NameError` every tick) and **−2,193.9** / −2,193.9, market maker −4.2 / −4.3 and 40.8 / 16.2; qwen3.8 market maker 2 12.0 / 1.6. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **The best local bot alone is now deepseek-r1's second market maker,** 40.8 (56% of the template's 72.7). It has no fair beyond the mid: it quotes its whole room at the mid ∓ 1. Under `worse` no local bot passes gemma4's 17.1. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **phi4's bots barely trade:** three of four are within 5k of zero. Its repaired baseline runs, but tracks its positions in state it never updates, and breaches limits 50,244 times. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **The zoo's worst bot:** deepseek-r1's second baseline, a long-only 3-tick momentum taker that takes the whole best ask and sells the whole best bid back. In the template's market it crosses 971k units at −1.29 to −4.33 against the wall mid; the template makes 490.9 there. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **Repair turns:** two, both for code that crashed every tick. phi4's fix runs; deepseek-r1 returned the same missing import. The first attempts sent the validator's summary line without the traceback ("30000 errors in 30,000 ticks"), and deepseek-r1 answered with advice and no code. `local.py` now sends the traceback, and both turns were redone from the same first answers. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **Spread between samples again:** deepseek-r1's market makers 40.8 and −4.2, phi4's 37.4 and −0.5, qwen3.8's 35.4 and 12.0. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **The template beats all nine head to head under both modes.** In the field of 32 (`--local`) it makes 247.4 (186.5 in the field of 24). Both parts are simulator or mock-portal days (VANTA is LARGE_TICK, ±400 credits over five sessions), and the study hard-codes the suggestion (`smoothing` 0.54; `--suggest` reads 0.56 today). So a trend costs the quotes little only where fills are as optimistic as `all` or the market's own; under `worse` it turns a small gain into a loss, and where the large-tick and drift notes both fire, take-only (`make_size` 0) is the candidate to grid against the suggestion. **Re-run 2026-11-04** with the study reading `--suggest` live (`smoothing` 0.56) and `--suggest` adding that note for a large-tick product that drifts: in the market, resting, +83,368 (none) / +79,958 (trend up) / +81,967 (down) / +79,184 (shift); replayed under `worse`, −54 / −1,535 / −2,576 / −1,610. The `worse` gate removes nothing when the second mode's noise is 0.8-correlated with the first's, as it is when both replay the same trades (6.0% against 10.0% only if independent). `arena.grid --holdout 25%` (StepM on the first 75% of the blocks, the pick kept only if it also gains on the rest) brings it to 4.7% / 6.3% / 12.3% / 7.0% / 9.0%. <br>**2026-10-26, each resample studentized by its own omega** (Romano and Wolf's Algorithm 4.2 step 3: `arena.stats.bootstrap` reruns Hansen's kernel on every resample's column; `studentize="resample"`, now the default of SPA and StepM on both engines; `uv run python -m experiments.study.snooping_null_rates --engine rust`, about 5 minutes, which prints both ways, and `--trials 1000`, about 20): over the six null rows above, StepM names a noise cell in 6.0 / 6.0 / 7.7 / 7.7 / 8.0 / 6.0% of grids (6.0 / 5.9 / 8.3 / 6.3 / 6.6 / 5.9% over 1,000 grids a row), against 8.7 / 10.0 / 22.3 / 10.3 / 12.7 / 10.0% (8.5 / 10.7 / 20.3 / 8.4 / 10.0 / 10.7%) with the original's omega in every resample; SPA rejects in 5.3–7.7% (5.6–8.4%). The pick with `--holdout 25%`: 3.7–5.3% (3.1–4.4%), against 4.7–12.3% (4.1–12.4%). So most of the excess is gone, the AR(1) 0.8 row's above all, but the rates still sit 1–3 points over 5%. The price is power: one cell with a planted gain of 0.3 a block (about 0.2 of its sd) is named in 51% of grids rather than 62% (1,000 grids), and kept by the holdout pick in 35% rather than 47%. Real grids: `uv run python -m experiments.study.snooping_tests --engine rust` gives the same verdicts (Kelp: SPA^c p 0.00 at every block length, StepM the same three cells; Osmium: none; the ten null grids: SPA^c p 0.17–0.90, StepM none); `uv run python -m experiments.day.selection_rules --part 1 --engine rust` makes the same picks but one ("Selection rules on unseen days"). | **Learning `make_edge` online** (Fernandez-Tapia: a market maker that learns its half-spread from its own fills, so it would fit whatever fill model the exchange has; formerly untested) | 2026-10-26, `uv run python -m experiments.study.online_edge` (3 min, Python engine). The learner is `learn_edge` in `python/bots/research/online_edge.py`, a copy of the template, off by default. **The rule:** windows of 100 ticks in pairs, one quoting at the centre `e`, the other probing `e + 0.5` or `e − 0.5` (in turn); each passive fill (at one of our last three ticks' quote prices) scores its markout 5 ticks later; after each pair `e` moves 0.5 toward the probe if the probe scored more, and stays otherwise, within 0.5–4. Six fill models: `all`, `worse`, `worse` with a 0.5 fee, resting with `queue=prob`, one tick late, and resting against a copy of the template (`war`). **The learner's own settings were chosen on P3 round 1 day −2 alone:** 36 settings (two designs, probing both sides every pair or the centre against one probe, × window 100 / 250 / 500 × markout horizon 5 / 20 / 100 × step 0.25 / 0.5; the probe of 0.5 and the bounds fixed by reasoning), by the worst regret over the models (each model's best fixed edge less the learner, mean over the three starts, Kelp plus Resin). The pick scored 2,079 against 3,075 for the best fixed edge per product (Kelp 1, Resin 0.5–1); the both-sides design's best scored 3,274. The ranking is noisy: before a one-tick fix to the window count, the same grid put window 250 / horizon 100 first and today's pick fifth. A (product, size) class is informed on a day if its trades at extremes move at least 3 times the larger of the product's own reversion after a new extreme and its half-spread at 1,000 ticks, with 80% hits; chosen if so on both training days (leave one day out). <br>- **Size alone fails on Kelp and Squid Ink, as the audit found:** their 15-lot trades are 29–45 a day with a mean move at 1,000 ticks of −0.7 to +0.1 (Kelp, hit 33–42%) and −1.9 to +2.1 (Squid Ink, 43–58%); at an extreme the same size moves +1.0 / +7.0 (Kelp) and +39.8 / +59.8 / +15.5 (Squid Ink) on 2–3 trades a day. On Croissants size alone works: the 3-lot trades are 2 a day, all Olivia's, +17 to +23. <br>- **Held out:** Croissants' 3-lot class is chosen on every fold and flags exactly Olivia's 6 trades (precision 6/6, recall 6/6, +17 to +23). Kelp and Squid Ink are chosen only on the fold that holds out day 2 (day 2's classes miss the rule: Kelp's 15-lot move +1.0 against a bar of 4.5, Squid Ink's hits 50%), where Kelp flags 3 trades (2 of them Olivia's, +1.0) and Squid Ink 5 (1 hers, 40% hits): recall 2/7 and 1/7. <br>- **Copying the flags** (first by writing them into the trades as a name and copying it; since 2026-10-27 through the template's own `copy_sizes`, on trades with every name blanked, which gives the same numbers once it checks the extreme at the fair the trade printed against, not the next tick's: a buy at the low lifts the next fair off it, and the next-tick version made only +1.3k): +38.3k over plain market making on the product-days it chose (Croissants +55.1k, as the name gives; Kelp −10.5k, Squid Ink −6.2k on day 2). Copying Olivia by name on all 9 held-out product-days: +40.2k (Croissants +55.1k, Squid Ink +9.2k, Kelp −24.1k). <br>- **ABIDES** (`uv run python -m experiments.day.informed --data data/abides_named --days 1 --informed ValueAgent --engine rust`, after `uv run python -m experiments.abides_days generate --days 10 --names --out data/abides_named`: the same 10 days with the agents' types as buyer and seller): no class is chosen on any fold. Value agents' trades, on the side they took, move +12 to +28 cents their way at 1,000 ticks against a half-spread of 0.5, with 54–67% hits, over 7,600–8,300 trades a day (re-run 2026-10-28 on the regenerated days, R13; on the first, +11 to +37 and 53–65%): informed on average, not right on nearly every trade, and in no size class at extremes. Copying them by name loses 426–505k a day: `copy` turns the whole limit on each trade, which suits one trader with a few trades a day, not a crowd | `measured`: the detector recovers the name only where the informed trader has a size of its own (Croissants); on Kelp and Squid Ink three days are too few to choose her class held out; ABIDES's informed flow is a crowd the rule can't see | If the Arena's trades carry no names: `uv run python -m experiments.day.informed --data <folder> --days <days> --format qminers --bot <our bot>` on the download. Trade a chosen class with `copy` (it needs a filter in the template first: size and new extreme in STRATEGY) only if it is chosen on every held-out day and its move beats what market making makes there. A crowd that is right on average needs a flow signal instead (`experiments.day.predict`) |
| **A feed beyond the book as a fair term** (an audit's idea 5: for any numeric column besides the book, its level and change against the wall mid at the same tick and ahead, a leave-one-day-out fit, an event study) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.day.predict --part 1` (2 s): P3 round 4's observations file for MAGNIFICENT_MACARONS, read from the data folder (`arena.formats.prosperity` hands the bot an empty Observation). <br>- **The foreign bid and ask are the price:** their one-tick change correlates +0.88 with the wall mid's at the same tick on every day, and nothing ahead (+0.00 at 1–100 ticks). Fitted on two days, the wall mid moves b = 1.00 per unit of the foreign price on the third, with out-of-sample R² 0.76–0.80 tick by tick and 0.99 over 20 ticks; around their largest changes (6+) the wall mid moves +6.4 at the same tick and nothing before (−0.2 at −5). <br>- **Sugar:** +0.55 at the same tick, b = 7.7–9.3 a unit, R² 0.29–0.42 held out; nothing ahead. **Sunlight:** −0.02 at the same tick, −0.12 at 100 ticks ahead but not on every day; after its largest rises the wall mid falls 13.8 over 100 ticks (500 events). Tariffs and transport fees change a few times a day and carry nothing. <br>- **ABIDES's fundamental** (the answer key, read as a feed for an upper bound; `--data data/abides --days 1 --part 1`): +0.01 at the same tick, rising to +0.05 at 100 ticks on every day; over 100 ticks the wall mid moves b = 0.24–0.26 of the fundamental's change (R² +0.03 to +0.14); after its largest jumps (8+ cents) +0.2 at once, +1.1 by 20 ticks, +4.5 by 100 (re-run 2026-10-28 on the regenerated days, R13): the market follows the value slowly | `measured`: a Prosperity feed is a same-tick fair input (the foreign price one for one; sugar about 8 a unit), never a forecast | If the Arena's state or download has any field beyond the book: `--part 1 --data <folder> --days <days>` (Prosperity-layout files beside the prices; another format needs its loader to keep the field). A column with R² well above 0 held out and b steady across the days becomes a `fair += b × (feed − level)` term; one that leads (a lead column with *) is a forecast, the next row |
| **A linear forecast from the book and the flow** (edge_ideas item 6: leave one day out, ridge, the wall mid's move 1/5/20/100 ticks ahead on ρ, ρ3, adds, removals, OFI, signed trades, momentum over 1/5/20/100 ticks, the time of day and the other products' 5-tick returns; Sitaru et al.'s rule, trade only when the forecast beats the half-spread) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.day.predict --part 2` (12 s; P3 round 1, P4 rounds 1–2) and `uv run python -m experiments.day.predict --data data/abides --days 1 --part 2` (14 s). **Prosperity: no forecast beats the half-spread per sd.** <br>- Resin R² 0.43 and Pepper Root 0.45 out of sample at every horizon, Osmium 0.38 at 1 tick down to 0.05 at 100, all positive on every held-out day: the wall mid's bounce back to its level, read from ρ3 (+0.14 to +0.58 a sd) and negative momentum. One sd of the forecast moves 0.33 (Resin), 0.70 (Osmium) and 0.80 ticks (Pepper Root) against half-spreads of 4, 8 and 7. Kelp's R² is 0.08 at 1 tick and 0 at 100; Squid Ink's below 0 on some days. <br>- Sitaru's rule still trades profitably on the rare ticks where the forecast beats the half-spread: Resin 2.3–2.5% of ticks, +0.8 to +1.0 a trade on every day; Osmium 0.1%, +1 to +9; Pepper Root at 100 ticks every tick (its drift, which `target` 80 already holds). Those are the book crossing the fair, the template's takes. <br>- **ABIDES: the trigger fires from 5 ticks ahead.** R² above 0 on all 10 held-out days at every horizon (0.02 pooled at 1 tick, 0.04 at 5, 0.09 at 20, 0.14 at 100), and one sd of the forecast moves 0.19, 0.56, 1.55 and 5.31 ticks against a half-spread of 0.5: not at 1 tick. Sitaru's rule trades 0.4–1.1% of ticks at 1 tick ahead for +0.09 to +0.55 a trade, 24–27% at 5 for +0.14 to +0.46, 64–69% at 20 for +0.79 to +1.25, 88–91% at 100 for +3.8 to +5.5, on every day. The weights at 100 ticks are steady across folds: momentum over 100 ticks +3.8 to +4.0 a sd, ρ3 +3.2 to +3.4, ρ −1.0 to −1.1, 20-tick momentum +0.5 to +0.8; the signed trades are not among the largest. ABIDES's price continues (its momentum agents) where Prosperity's reverts. **Corrected 2026-10-28** (the fourth audit's R8): the signed trades were the tick's own prints, which come after its snapshot and reach a bot a tick later; at 1 tick that was most of the answer (R² 0.15, 0.53 a sd, so "fires at every horizon", Sitaru's rule on 19–21% of ticks, the trades' weight +1.1). The feature is now the tick before's; Prosperity's numbers move in the third decimal. The days are the ones regenerated on 2026-10-28 (R13, `experiments.abides_days`) | `measured`: on Prosperity the forecast is the wall mid's bounce, worth no more than the takes the template makes; on ABIDES a continuation forecast beats the spread on every held-out day | On the download: `--part 2 --data <folder> --days <days>`. If a product's move per sd beats its half-spread with R² > 0 on every held-out day, build the `linear` fair (the row below) for it |

| **The template inside ABIDES's market** (the deferred test: ABIDES's agents see our orders, so takes meet impact and momentum traders react; `experiments.study.abides_market`, the template run as an ABIDES agent once a second) | 2026-10-27, re-run 2026-10-28, `uv run python -m experiments.study.abides_market --days 10 [--set PARAMS.ABM.lin_rho3=3 --set PARAMS.ABM.lin_momentum=0.5] [--set PARAMS.ABM.gate_move=2 --set PARAMS.ABM.gate_window=50] [--resting] [--see-own] [--options ...]`, seeds 1–10, against the idle run of the same seed replayed in our engine (`all` / `worse` / resting). **Corrected 2026-10-28** (the fourth audit's R14 and R15): the agent sent its new orders with the cancels, sized on its position alone, so last second's quotes could fill too and the position reached up to three times the limit (|300|) on 8 of 10 seeds, which the replay never allows; and it saw its own quotes in the book, which a replayed day never shows. Now its new orders wait for the cancels and fit the room beside the position and the live orders (the largest |position| in every run below is 100), and its book leaves out its own orders. **As committed: −230,284 in the market (orders living a second) and −127,564 keeping its quotes**, against −21,798 / −57,872 / −362,428 replayed: worse than the replay's `worse` per second, better than its resting, losing on 9 of 10 seeds (6 of 10 resting). **The linear fair: +1,274,739 in the market, positive on all 10 seeds (94,994 to 205,475), and +1,267,377 keeping its quotes**, against 1,220,893 / 1,175,092 / 1,121,620 replayed: 104% of the replay's `all` (per seed 0.66–1.95: the same seed, not the same day, since our messages shift when the agents query the oracle, and the fundamental parts from second 64). The gate alone: +652,748 against 765,448 / 581,898 / 652,707. **What the two flaws cost:** with its own quotes left in its book (`--see-own`, the limit kept), as committed −642,291 and the linear fair +1,103,771; the first run's −617,092 and +1,066,496 (and "87% of the replay") were mostly that, the template's fair following its own full-size quote. **Other RMSC04 setups** (`--options`, the same 10 seeds; in the market / replay `all`, as committed then the linear fair): **no momentum agents** (`'{"num_momentum_agents": 0}'`) −285,449 / 32,920 and **+1,304,626** / 1,347,556; **300 value agents** (`'{"num_value_agents": 300}'`) −441,021 / −59,965 and **+1,489,360** / 1,534,327; **20 momentum agents** (`'{"num_momentum_agents": 20}'`, the most the default's value agents hold) −1,346,552 / −1,282,250 and **+1,753,606** / 1,509,255; **50 momentum with 250 value agents** −1,626,108 / −1,087,382 and **+1,385,506** / 1,512,741; **RMSC03-like** (`'{"num_noise_agents": 5000, "num_momentum_agents": 25, "fund_vol": 0.001}'`, seeds 3, 5 and 6 broken and left out) −3,159,076 / −4,900,298 and **+13,386,488** / 5,010,029 (inflated by seeds 1 and 2, +2.9M and +5.6M against 0.6M and 0.4M replayed). In each of these setups the committed template loses on every seed in the market and the linear fair makes money on every one. More momentum: 50 momentum agents against the default 102 value agents is a broken market (seed 1's price ran from 100,000 to 880,000 and down to 6,000, the fundamental near 100,000, a side of the book empty a third of the seconds); so is 25 on some seeds and 35 on every one tried, so the momentum agents need value agents to match. `abides_market` flags a broken day (its idle run's mid more than 50% off the fundamental, or a side empty over 1% of the seconds; RMSC04's own days: 0.1% and 0.1%) and leaves it out of the total. So the linear fair's edge doesn't come from the momentum agents, and inside the market it holds what the replay says. The template also gets no market trades there (`{}`), so `copy` and `copy_sizes` would be off (unused by these settings). | `measured`: in an agent market with momentum the replay's ranking holds and its numbers are about right once the bot keeps its limit and doesn't read its own quotes; the linear fair's edge is real, not an artefact of the recording | On the day, if the market reacts (probe 17): believe a replay's ranking more than its numbers; the linear fair and the gate stay the candidates where the trigger fires. Where cancels aren't instant, size new orders on the position plus the orders still live, as the ABIDES agent now does |

(`exhaustion_gate` reads each call's trades against the call before's book, `quote_markouts` against the same tick's.) | **Skewing on correlated products' inventory too (Guéant's `Γ`)** (`references/wiki/concepts/many-products-architecture.md`; "Fills arriving in several family members at once" above: on P4 round 5 the template's fills cluster across a family and its members' positions correlate +0.7 to +0.99; formerly untested) | 2026-11-02, `uv run python -m experiments.study.family_skew 5 --data p4 --grid 'DEFAULTS.skew=0,2,5' --grid 'DEFAULTS.family_weight=0,0.5,1' --modes all,worse --holdout last` (about 5 min, the Python engine): P4 round 5, every product at the data's limit (10) and DEFAULTS otherwise, a family the products sharing their name's first word. Each product's `target` is moved against the rest of its family: `family_weight` × Σ (the others' position over their limit) × its own limit, clamped to the limit; `skew` quotes against it and `clear` crosses toward it, so at weight 1 a member skews as if it held the family's position. `all` / `worse`: <br>- **No skew:** the bot as is 456,360 / 444,766; with the family term alone (only `clear` acts on it) 422,185 / 413,590 at 0.5 and 430,590 / 422,085 at 1. <br>- **`skew` 2:** own inventory 404,963 / 255,810; with the family 234,334 / 143,878 (0.5) and 228,800 / 172,568 (1). **`skew` 5:** 225,042 / 189,798; 65,580 / 72,206 and 76,137 / 66,535. <br>- The bot as is wins on every measure and on two of three held-out days; StepM at 4% names no cell (SPA p 0.547). Own-inventory skew costs, as on P3 ("`skew` against Kelp's long position" above), and the family's adds to the cost: the members' prices move independently (or against each other in Pebbles and Snackpacks), so their clustered inventories carry no risk worth giving fills up for. Prosperity has no family of positively correlated products that the template makes markets in (baskets and options have their own modes) | `dropped here` | Only where `experiments.day.family_fills`' netting table on the download shows a family's moves correlated positively and own `skew` wins a grid (an inventory cost, a worse close): then `experiments.study.family_skew` on the day's data |

ABIDES: 0.46% of 191,500, up to 35 ticks out (its hidden depth). So a quote 20, 50 or 100 from the wall mid fills nothing in any mode. The takers that filled the teams' far quotes traded against those quotes live; the published days hold no trade where no level was shown *(inferred)* | `measured`: not testable on replays; the template has no empty-side rule (`TODO.md`, "The edge plan", deferred) | On the day: probe 14 is the test; `structure`'s last fingerprint column on the download says whether its replays could value a far quote at all |

| **A spoof tell from the data** (Tao et al.: the imbalance just before market trades against its usual distribution; `references/wiki/sources/detecting-spoofing-strategies.md`; `harder-rounds` D3; formerly untested) | - **The scalar, 2026-10-27** (R6's first half: Tao et al.'s condition (3.4)/(5.1), `max_k 2ρµ+(1 − ı̄)ı̄w_k − Q_kρ − ν_k`, as `arena.diagnose`'s `spoof` column with its inputs `spoof_mu`, `spoof_size`, `spoof_q0..2`, `spoof_nu0..2` in the CSV and a `spoofable` note above 0; `uv run python -m arena.diagnose R --data pN --csv out.csv` for every P2–P4 round, and `1 --data data/abides`): on the products the template makes markets in, the next move goes against the depth imbalance (µ+ −0.5 to −0.8 ticks a unit of 2ı − 1 on P4 Osmium and Pepper Root, −3.3 to −7.9 on P2/P3 Resin, Amethysts, Kelp, Starfruit, Squid Ink; −18 and −44 on P4 Velvetfruit and Hydrogel), so no spoof pays (scores −0.01 to −0.79). 15 of 136 product-rounds score above 0, all on a book whose depth leans toward where the mid goes next: P2's gift basket (+0.14, µ+ 7.7), P3's Picnic Basket 1 (+0.10 to +0.11, µ+ 5.5–5.7), Macarons (+0.17, µ+ 10) and some volcanic vouchers (+0.01 to +0.07). The ABIDES days: µ+ +0.026, orders 19% of the 3-level depth, reaching level 0's end 16% of the time: −0.01, just short (the same on the days regenerated 2026-10-28). <br>- **The detector's baseline, 2026-11-02** (R6's second half, its inputs: `arena.diagnose`'s `spoof_before` and `spoof_snap`, in the CSV; the same runs): the Wasserstein distance between the 3-level bid share just before market buys and on every tick (the ask's share for sells, weighted by their counts), and between the share before them and after. With no spoofer anywhere: 0.000–0.019 on the products the template makes markets in (ABIDES 0.008), at most 0.001 on P4 round 5's 50 products, 0.012–0.077 on the baskets, Macarons, P2's coupon and P4's vouchers, and 0.18–0.27 on P3's volcanic vouchers. The two distances agree within 0.004 on every product, since the books are redrawn every tick and the book after a trade is any tick's: there, orders come when their own side of the book is heavier, the generator's lean, which reads exactly as a spoof would. A `--vs` spoofer can't show the tell on replays: the recorded takers don't react to its orders and the recorded book doesn't hold them; on a synthetic day a lean timed on the trades reads 0.17 / 0.25 and an untimed one about 0 (`python/tests/test_diagnose.py`) | `measured`: not on the market-making products; the positive scores read an association in a replay (the generator draws book and price around one value), while a spoof pays only where other traders' quotes react to the book, which a recording can't show. The detector reads a spoofer only against the product's own baseline, never against 0 | On the day: the column per product on the download; where it is above 0 and the market is shared, run the detector half (the imbalance before trades against after) on the first resting upload's book. Read `spoof_before` / `spoof_snap` on the download as that product's baseline, and the same distances on the book our bot saw (probe 22) against it |
| **Optimal entry and exit bands for a basket's spread** (Cartea et al.'s Ornstein–Uhlenbeck bands in place of the template's band in sds; `references/wiki/concepts/baskets-and-fixed-sums.md`; formerly untested) | 2026-11-02, `uv run python -m experiments.day.baskets --engine rust` (its part 4, about 25 s): per held-out day, κ and σ from an AR(1) of the other two days' spreads less each day's mean, the level as in part 2 (the other days' mean), c the basket's `clear_edge`, and the double-sided triggers of Cartea et al. ch. 11 (the long entry is the short exit, (11.4), and the short entry the long exit, (11.2); the solver reproduces the book's table to four decimals, `python/tests/test_experiments.py`). The model discounts the spread's whole value, so they are solved for the spread less its level, which makes them symmetric: `level_band` the long exit, `level_exit` None, no hedge. The spreads revert with a half-life of 750–1,030 ticks on P3's baskets and 190–250 on P2's gift basket. Held out, in thousands over three days, against part 2's k-sd grid (70.7 on P3 round 2, 294.3 on P2 round 3): <br>- **The urgency ρ sets the band, and nothing in the data fixes it:** at one over a day's ticks the bands are 1.6–1.7 sd (P3) and 2.1–2.2 sd (P2), and make 86.3 and 170.6; at ten times that, 0.8 and 1.3 sd, 78.9 and 240.7; at a tenth, 2.4–2.9 sd, 5.7 and 57.3. <br>- So the fitted bands win on one dataset and lose 42% on the other; with ρ gridded they are the k grid by another name. The model has no position limit and no day end, which is what makes a narrower band pay here | `dropped here`: the k-sd band grid by held-out days stays; part 4 stays in the script as a cross-check | On the day, for a basket: part 4 next to part 2 on the download; trust it only where it agrees with the held-out grid |
| **Classifying market trades by co-occurrence across a basket and its legs** (Petit et al.; Lu et al.'s four types; `references/wiki/sources/etf-trade-flow-decomposition.md`: a basket trade with trades in its legs around it is arbitrage flow, one without a directional bet *(their reading, inferred for us)*; formerly untested) | 2026-11-02, `uv run python -m experiments.study.cross_product_timing` (its section 3, P3 round 2's baskets and P2 round 3's gift basket, all three days): each tick's basket market trades, signed against the tick's book, "with legs" when a leg traded within a tick of it (49–63% of the basket's trade ticks), else "alone"; the basket's mid move over the next h ticks in the trade's direction, with its standard error. <br>- **Both classes move the basket the same way:** +0.5 to +1.3 ticks at h = 1 in each class (Picnic Basket 1 +1.05 ± 0.15 with legs, +1.26 ± 0.20 alone). At h = 20 the moves with legs stay (+0.89 to +1.77) and those alone fade on P3 (+0.40 and +0.25), but each gap is within one standard error (Picnic Basket 1 +1.22 ± 0.62 against +0.40 ± 0.83; the gift basket +1.77 ± 1.15 against +1.37 ± 1.58) and the days disagree in sign. <br>- **None pays a taker:** every mean move is below half the basket's spread (2.5 on Picnic Basket 2, 4.5 and 5.5 on the others) | `measured`: no flow class to trade on Prosperity's baskets; the generator's trades in a family carry the same information whether or not the legs trade | Only if an Arena round has a basket with enough trades: section 3's split on the download (the study is Prosperity-only; its `signed_volume` and classes are a few lines to point at another day) |

That is about the ½Γ S² (σ²realised − σ²implied) of 200 lots at the money with the 1,000-tick realised vol (2.0–6.2k a day; with the tick-level vol it would be 9.5–12.1k, so most of that vol is noise a hedge doesn't collect: the audit's round 8, R18a). Unhedged it is a bet on the rock (−78.6k). On 9750–10250 hedged −29.7k (one day −41.2k), not fully hedged: the wanted rock hedge (413–580 lots on two days) is over the rock's limit of 400, and `hedge_target` clamps it; short and hedged −45.0k (10000) and −8.3k. <br>- Next to it, taking each voucher's fast deviation from the smile makes 528.8k on the same limit ("Options: trading implied-volatility deviations from a fitted smile" above). The table mostly says to join the best at a wide spread, which `all` credits in full and a queue doesn't | `dropped here`: the one-tick improvement stays; the table is `all`'s optimism | On the day: `fill_curve`'s part 3 on the download only next to its half 2 (the queue share from the first upload); trade a per-spread distance only if the portal fills like `all` |
| **Fitting `arena.sim` to the download, then tuning on many generated days** (`TODO.md`, "The edge plan", deferred; `edge_ideas.md` item 3; formerly untested) | 2026-11-02, `uv run python -m experiments.day.sim_fit` (new; the controls P3 round 1 Kelp and P4 round 1 Osmium, `--evals 300 --out data/fitted --generate 20`, about 2.5 min): thirteen moments per product from `arena.diagnose` and the data (spread, its share at one tick, wall size, the walls' distance apart, the wall mid's tick vol, reversion and jumps, the mid's lag-1 autocorrelation, trades a tick, their size, buy share, share inside the spread and share 2+ ticks from the wall mid), each scaled by its sd across the data's days; the nearest preset as the start; Nelder–Mead over ten of its parameters on two simulated days with fixed seeds, restarted twice from its best (the walls round to ticks, so the surface is flat in steps). <br>- **Corrected 2026-11-03** (the audit's round 8, R9 and R10): the first version had no moment for the walls' distance, no restarts, and its `--check` figures were from before two parameters were added; re-run, it recovered the half-spread at 2.01 for 2.5. And the transfer was judged on the top cell alone. <br>- **The check** (`--check --evals 300`: KELP with four parameters moved): distance 6.8; the half-spread 2.42 for 2.5, the noise takers' rate 0.158 for 0.15, the value takers' 0.041 for 0.04, the limit traders' 1.49 for 1.6, the buy share 0.49 for 0.5; the tick sd 0.32 for 0.25 (it trades against the limit traders' offsets: the moments identify the rates and the walls better than the fundamental's step). <br>- **Kelp fits** (distance 2.7, the buy share 0.35 found); **Osmium only partly** (287, from HYDROGEL_PACK): its mid's bounce (lag-1 −0.46 against −0.15 fitted) and its walls 20 apart (fitted 16) come from inner orders flickering on a lattice no preset agent makes. <br>- **Whether tuning carries over, by the whole ranking** (`uv run python -m arena.grid python/bots/template.py 1 --data data/fitted --grid 'PARAMS.KELP.make_edge=0.5,1,1.5,2,3,4' --grid 'PARAMS.KELP.take_edge=0.5,1,2' --modes all,worse --engine rust --out fitted.csv`, the same on `--data p3 --out real.csv`, then `uv run python -m experiments.day.sim_fit --compare real.csv fitted.csv --products KELP`; likewise Osmium on `--data p4`): on Kelp's own PnL the 18 cells rank alike, Spearman +0.97 under `all` and +0.93 under `worse`; the fitted best is second on the real days under `all` and the same cell under `worse`, at the real days' level (the top cell 29,950 a three days against 30,202). Osmium's don't: +0.16 / +0.13, its fitted best seventh on the real days. (The audit's "wide quotes earn 0–11k fitted against 54–59k real" compared the grids' totals, which hold Resin's 54k on the real days and Osmium on the fitted.) | `open`: a tuning ground for a product whose ranking carries over | On the day, per product: `sim_fit` on the download, a small grid on both the real and the fitted days, `--compare`; grid on the fitted days only where the ranking carries over (Spearman about 0.8 or more), otherwise the fit names what the presets lack |
| **A pretrained bar forecaster (Kronos) as a detector** (the user's lead, 2026-11-02: Kronos, `references/kronos`, a transformer pretrained on real exchanges' OHLCV bars, 1-minute to weekly (`references/SEARCH_LOG.md`, "Pretrained models"). It can't go in a bot (torch, 31 MB of weights or more, no book), so the question is whether, run beside the data, it sees structure in a product's prices that the simple fits miss) | 2026-11-02, `uv run python -m experiments.study.kronos_zeroshot setup` (once: its own environment and the pinned weights, about 1.3 GB), then `uv run python -m experiments.study.kronos_zeroshot` (10 min on an Apple M-series GPU: P3 and P4 round 1, every day, Kronos-mini and -small) and `--models base --days 0` (about 20 min). Bars of 10 ticks, 256 bars of context, an origin every 4 bars (185 a day), 10 sampled paths averaged, against AR(1), AR(5), a reversion to the 5-bar mean and `wall_rev`, a reversion to the wall mid, each fitted on the same 256 bars. R² against forecasting no move, 1 bar / 5 bars ahead, mean over 3 days, Kronos-small against `wall_rev`: <br>- **Never above the wall-mid reversion.** Resin +0.47/+0.48 against +0.48/+0.51 (a tie: Resin's bars bounce around a fixed fair), Osmium +0.14/+0.01 against +0.24/+0.10, Kelp +0.09/−0.12 against +0.34/+0.13. Mini is no better (Osmium +0.19/+0.00, Kelp +0.09/−0.05), and base on day 0 neither (Kelp +0.20/+0.11 against +0.42/+0.21, Osmium +0.10/−0.05 against +0.17/+0.11). AR(5) and the 5-bar mean, which see only prices as Kronos does, match or beat Kronos everywhere but Resin. <br>- **Worse than no forecast on Squid Ink** (−0.24/−0.08; mini −0.37/−0.15) where every fit is about 0, and **against the drift on Pepper Root**: −9.8/−4.8 against the fits' +0.5/+0.94, a sign hit rate of 6% at 5 bars against 99.5%. Kronos forecasts −6.8 ticks over 5 bars where the drift gives +5.0, a reversion to the window's mean, and its tokenizer alone (encode and decode the window, no model) misses the last close by 3.9 ticks on average, more than a bar's move: the window is normalised by its own sd (about 74 ticks over a 256-bar trend), so the 20-bit token spends its resolution on the trend (Kelp's round trip, sd 1–3, is 0.25). <br>- Cost: about 8 s a product-day for mini, 32 s for small and 140–340 s for base, against no cost for the fits. | `dropped here`: on Prosperity it restates the wall mid's bounce at best, adds noise on a random walk and reads a drift as a reversal; and it can't trade (stdlib bot) | Only if the download looks like a real market's bars rather than Prosperity's (`experiments.day.premise` agent-like, and `wall_rev`'s reversion weak): `--data <folder> --round R` if the files are in the Prosperity layout. A product where Kronos beats `wall_rev` on every day is a lead for `experiments.day.predict`, not a fair to ship |

## Benchmark: the template against the published P4 traders

The teams' own files in our engine (2026-10-13; commands on each team's page under `references/wiki/sources/`). **These are the teams' traders on the days they were built on, so they overstate what they did live.** DTU's 3-day backtests were 135,842 (round 3), 116,090 (round 4) and 501,498 (round 5); its live single days were +3,686, +25,351 and +702,835.

**Rounds 1–2, PnL per day.** The template is level with DTU's round 2 trader and 6–9k a day behind the best:

| Trader | Round 1 | Round 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Template (Pepper Root `target` 80) | 93.9–96.8k | 96.0–96.7k |
| DTU, round 2 trader | 95.5–97.0k | 97.4–98.7k |
| DU, `round2.py` | 100.6–102.1k | 103.0–105.0k |
| Baguette Fromage, `Phase1.py` | 101.9–102.8k | 104.2–105.6k |

**Rounds 3–5, PnL over each round's 3 days, in thousands.** "Template" here is plain market making around `wall_mid` on every product: for each product, `--set 'PARAMS.<PRODUCT>={"limit": <limit>, "fair": "wall_mid", "make_edge": 1}'`. Rounds 3 and 4 share days 1 and 2, so they hold 4 distinct days, not 6.

| Round | Products | Template | DTU | Baguette Fromage | DU |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | VEV vouchers (options) | 11.6 | 47.2 | 604.3 | 366.9 |
| 3 | Hydrogel | 29.5 | 55.5 | 148.8 | 64.8 |
| 3 | Velvetfruit (the vouchers' underlying) | 14.6 | 33.2 | 98.1 | 73.2 |
| 4 | VEV vouchers | 11.3 | 40.0 | 620.2 | 382.5 |
| 4 | Hydrogel | 27.4 | 47.2 | 161.8 | 67.0 |
| 4 | Velvetfruit | 4.9 | 28.9 | 106.5 | 74.7 |
| 5 | Robots (the round-number jumps) | 11.1 | 9.2 | 508.5 | 308.6 |
| 5 | Oxygen shakes | 76.7 | 167.1 | 147.5 | 62.0 |
| 5 | Pebbles (fixed sum) | 33.7 | 54.0 | 73.6 | 56.2 |
| 5 | Microchips | 20.3 | 45.6 | 51.5 | 46.2 |
| 5 | The other six families | 316.0 | 225.6 | 310.0 | 253.4 |
| | **Total, rounds 3 / 4 / 5** | 55.7 / 43.6 / 457.8 | 135.8 / 116.1 / 501.5 | 851.2 / 888.6 / 1,091.0 | 505.0 / 524.3 / 726.5 |

What it says, as priorities for strategy work:
1. **Options** are the largest gap: about 610k against 11k on the vouchers.
2. **Fading the round-number jumps** (the Robots in round 5): 309–509k against 11k. Almost all of it is ROBOT_DISHES on day 4, the only product-day of the round in its jumping regime ("Fading jumps that snap back", above).
3. **The spot products of rounds 3–4** (Hydrogel, Velvetfruit): 100–160k each for Baguette Fromage against 5–30k.
4. **Plain market making holds its own** on the other six round 5 families: 316k against 226–310k for the teams. It leads on Galaxy Sounds and Panels and trails mainly on Sleep Pods (54k against Baguette Fromage's 75k).

**Since 2026-10-13 the template trades rounds 3–4 with its level mode and the vouchers following Velvetfruit** (PARAMS tuned on these days, so in-sample): round 3 854k (Hydrogel 115k, Velvetfruit 96k, vouchers 642k), round 4 844k (123k, 91k, 631k), level with Baguette Fromage's 851k and 889k (re-run 2026-10-13 after the review's `level_exit` fix: round 3 was 860k, round 4 832k, held out 884k). Scored on days it wasn't tuned on, 879k over the four distinct days (`experiments.day.p4_level_template`, `experiments.day.p4_vouchers_template`). Under `--resting` the four days make 1,153k against 1,157k (`uv run python -m arena.backtest python/bots/template.py 3 4-3 --data p4 --resting`): it trades by crossing the spread, so the fill model barely matters. Priorities 1 and 3 are covered on P4; the Robots are next.

**Since 2026-10-13 the template's `jump` mode covers priority 2** (in-sample): switched on for every round 5 product on top of plain market making, it makes 958.8k over the three days against 457.8k without it, Baguette Fromage's 1,091.0k and DU's 726.5k (`experiments.day.p4_jumps`, "Fading jumps that snap back", above). What's left of the gap to Baguette Fromage is spread across the other families.

## Not yet tested

| Idea | Where it comes from | Test, and where it would go |
|---|---|---|
| Stacking quotes at several levels to hold queue places (Chung et al.: −2.13 to +2.33 a day for a simple rule) | `references/wiki/sources/order-stacking-mm.md` | Only if the Arena's orders rest with time priority (probes 1 and 11): `--resting` with the template quoting one extra level a side. The plain ladder (`make_levels`) is tested above, "Laddered quotes"; holding queue places this way needs time priority |
| Round 1 as the prepared team's largest PnL lead, if the total is weighted raw PnL (the user, 2026-11-02) | `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "Round weights, unconfirmed" (10/20/30/40%, from unused site strings) and "How is PnL scored?"; the site calls round 1 the "forgiving market". If the total is Σ weight × raw PnL, a round's lead counts in its own units: in round 1 most teams are still learning the API while our tooling grids a simple market, so our PnL lead there may be the largest of the four, and even at 10% it can outweigh later rounds' leads if PnL scales are similar. If rounds are normalised (to the best team, the field's spread) or ranked, a large win counts little more than a narrow one. **If PnL carries over** (the user, 2026-11-02: Qminers' 2025 hackathon did, its bankroll compounding across the finals rounds, as in 2024, `references/wiki/sources/qminers-hackathon-2024.md`): a carried-over score is the weighted case above; carried-over capital or limits would compound, so round 1's profit would also buy capacity in every later round, and a round-1 loss would shrink it. The 2024 winner, on a compounding bankroll, staked a quarter of it at three times its Kelly fraction for the first 1,000 bets, then folded it back in. In 2025 (the user, 2026-11-02) a team that lost its default 1,000 in a round was lent 1,000 to play the next, repaid at the end: the loss is capped at the stake while a win compounds, a convex payoff, where seeking variance in round 1 is right | Once the Technical Rules give the combination: under weighted raw PnL, run round 1 for expected PnL (every product traded, full limits where the grid holds, the grid's best by `pnl` among the cells that beat the bot as is under the playground's mode, and the round's submissions spent on PnL rather than probes after the first validated upload), with risk cheap at 10%; under normalised or ranked scoring, keep the usual pick; if capital or limits carry over, push round 1 harder still, but size it so a bad round 1 can't cut later rounds' capacity by more than the expected gain buys (the 2024 winner's aggressive-early, normal-later rule); if a busted stake is lent back as in 2025, the cut can't happen, so take the most variance the grid's cells offer in round 1 (the full limit, the widest-swinging cell that still beats the bot as is). Check on round 1's leaderboard how far the field is behind (`experiments.day.leaderboard_curves --read`), and score the choice with `experiments.day.standings`' four combinations |
| The portal's whole metrics set as an engine fingerprint, not only its PnL | `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "How is PnL scored?" (2026-10-26); the site's `--metrics` fields | On the first upload, match `cash + inventory × markPrice − breachPenalty`, fills, volume, the four \|position\| quantiles and the three `pnlSe` guesses; compare the results ZIP's fills/orders with `--out`. Done when the settings that match every figure are in the round's handover note |
| The leaderboard's cross-team PnL total, clock and field | `experiments.day.leaderboard_curves --read` (2026-10-26); `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "Is the market shared…" | After round 1, read the total across teams (flat vs rising), the `elapsedS` clock (length, spacing, resets) and the field's distribution and top drawdowns; write what each changed in the next bot into the handover note |
| Calibrating the queue model on fills | An audit's gap list (2026-10-26); `--queue back|prob|front` brackets the queue, nothing fits it; `references/wiki/sources/queue-reactive-model.md` (uniform cancels overstate fills) | On the day only: the first resting upload's fills (the results ZIP, `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "How is PnL scored?") against `--resting` under each `--queue` and `power`; keep the closest, as for the playground's mode |
| Pro-rata matching | `references/wiki/sources/pro-rata-microstructure.md` (Guilbaud & Pham, mapped); `ON_THE_DAY.md`, probe 11 | Only if probe 11 shows pro-rata: an engine mode that splits each trade at a price among resting orders by size, then `make_size` re-gridded (size buys a share, not only exposure) |
| A Kalman fair: a local-level (or local linear trend) filter with its own error bar, handling gaps | `TODO.md`, "The edge plan", deferred; `references/wiki/sources/spoofing-abm.md` has the predict and update steps (Wang & Wellman p. 3) | Only if the Kalman-implied `smoothing` (an EMA, the filter's steady state) falls short on the ABIDES days: a gap, a trend, or noise that changes through the day. A template fair mode with its twin, graded against `smoothing` on `KELP_NOISY`, Osmium and the ABIDES days <br>2026-10-27: on the ABIDES days the implied `smoothing` is 0 and the grid's best 0.1–0.2 (+40k under `all` on the days regenerated 2026-10-28, +67k on the first, "The Kalman-implied smoothing" above): the gap comes from moves that continue, not from gaps or a trend, so a Kalman fair would not close it |
| An online table of the adverse move per book state (ρ tercile × spread bucket, each pooled with its mirror), posting a side only where `make_edge` beats it | `TODO.md`, "The edge plan", deferred (R12); Cartea, Donnelly & Jaimungal (`references/wiki/sources/order-book-signals.md`); harder-rounds B4 | Only if the ABIDES or LOBSTER days show negative markouts: a research copy on P4 rounds 1–2 (expect no change), `KELP_NOISY` and the ABIDES days under `all`/`worse`/`--resting`. The online-edge row's caveat: markouts aren't PnL in a shared market |
